 9 Poincaré and Klainerman-Sobolev inequalities 

 
 We now state the following useful version of the Poincaré
inequality. 
 
 
 Lemma 9.1 . 
 
 Let f f be a smooth function.
Then for any γ > − 1 / 2 , γ ≠ 1 / 2 \gamma>-1/2,\gamma\neq 1/2 and any positive t t 
 

 
 (9.1) 
 
 ∫ R 3 | f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ≤ C ​ ∫ S ( t + 1 ) | f | 2 ​ 𝑑 S + C ​ ∫ R 3 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 ​ γ \int\limits_{R^{3}}\frac{|f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\leq C\int\limits_{S_{(t+1)}}|f|^{2}\,dS+C\int\limits_{R^{3}}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2\gamma}} 
 
 
 provided that the left hand side is bounded.
Here S ( t + 1 ) S_{(t+1)} is the sphere of radius t + 1 t+1 
and r = | x | r=|x| . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Using polar coordinates x = r ​ ω x=r\omega we write 
 

 
 
 | f ( r , ω ) | 2 − | f ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 = − 2 ∫ r t + 1 ∂ r f ( ρ , ω ) ⋅ f ( ρ , ω ) d ρ |f(r,\omega)|^{2}-|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}=-2\int_{r}^{t+1}\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)\cdot f(\rho,\omega)\,d\rho 
 
 
 Hence 
 

 
 
 | f ⁡ ( r , ω ) | 2 ​ r 2 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + 2 ​ ∫ r t + 1 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ , if r ≤ t + 1 . |f(r,\omega)|^{2}r^{2}\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}(t+1)^{2}+2\int_{r}^{t+1}|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)|\,|f(\rho,\omega)|\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho,\qquad\text{if}\quad r\leq t+1. 
 
 
 Therefore multiplying by ( 1 + | t − r | ) − 2 − 2 ​ γ (1+|t-r|)^{-2-2\gamma} and
integrating with respect to r r from 0 0 to t + 1 t+1 : 
 

 
 
 ∫ 0 t + 1 | f ⁡ ( r , ω ) | 2 ​ r 2 ​ d ​ r ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ \displaystyle\int_{0}^{{t+1}}\!\!\frac{|f(r,\omega)|^{2}\,r^{2}\,dr}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}} 
 ≲ ∫ 0 t + 1 | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 ​ d ​ r ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ + ∫ 0 t + 1 ∫ r t + 1 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ ​ 𝑑 r \displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t+1}\!\frac{|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}\,dr}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}+\int_{0}^{t+1}\int_{r}^{t+1}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho\,dr 
 
 
 
 
 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ∫ 0 t + 1 ∫ 0 ρ | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ​ 𝑑 r ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ \displaystyle\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t+1}\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\,dr\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho 
 
 
 
 
 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ∫ 0 t + 1 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ \displaystyle\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t+1}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-\rho|)^{1+2\gamma}}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho 
 
 
 
 
 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ( ∫ 0 t + 1 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | 2 ​ ρ 2 ​ d ​ ρ ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 2 ​ γ ) 1 / 2 ​ ( ∫ 0 t + 1 | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | 2 ​ ρ 2 ​ d ​ ρ ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ) 1 / 2 , \displaystyle\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}(t+1)^{2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t+1}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)|^{2}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho}{(1+|t-\rho|)^{2\gamma}}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{0}^{t+1}\frac{|f(\rho,\omega)|^{2}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho}{(1+|t-\rho|)^{2+2\gamma}}\Big)^{1/2}, 
 
 
 where we first changed the order of integration and then
used Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
It therefore follows that 
 

 
 
 ∫ 0 t + 1 | f ⁡ ( r , ω ) | 2 ​ r 2 ​ d ​ r ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ∫ 0 t + 1 | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | 2 ​ ρ 2 ​ d ​ ρ ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 2 ​ γ \int_{0}^{{t+1}}\!\!\frac{|f(r,\omega)|^{2}\,r^{2}\,dr}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t+1}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)|^{2}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho}{(1+|t-\rho|)^{2\gamma}} 
 
 
 and if we also integrate over the angular variables we get 
 

 
 
 ∫ | x | ≤ ( t + 1 ) | f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ≲ ∫ S ( t + 1 ) | f | 2 ​ 𝑑 S + ∫ | x | ≤ ( t + 1 ) | ∂ r f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 ​ γ \int\limits_{|x|\leq(t+1)}\frac{|f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\lesssim\int\limits_{S_{(t+1)}}|f|^{2}dS+\int\limits_{|x|\leq(t+1)}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2\gamma}} 
 
 
 On the other hand, if we instead integrate from t + 1 t+1 to 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2(t+1) we similarly obtain 
 

 
 
 ∫ t + 1 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | f ⁡ ( r , ω ) | 2 ​ r 2 ​ d ​ r ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ \displaystyle\int_{t+1}^{{2(t+1)}}\!\!\frac{|f(r,\omega)|^{2}\,r^{2}\,dr}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}} 
 ≲ ∫ t + 1 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 ​ d ​ r ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ + ∫ t + 1 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) ∫ t + 1 r | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ ​ 𝑑 r \displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t+1}^{2(t+1)}\!\frac{|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}\,dr}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}+\int_{t+1}^{2(t+1)}\!\!\int_{t+1}^{r}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho\,dr 
 
 
 
 
 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ∫ t + 1 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) ∫ ρ 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ​ 𝑑 r ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ \displaystyle\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}+\int_{t+1}^{2(t+1)}\int_{\rho}^{2(t+1)}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\,dr\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho 
 
 
 
 
 ≲ | f ⁡ ( t + 1 , ω ) | 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) 2 + ∫ t + 1 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | ∂ r f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ​ | f ⁡ ( ρ , ω ) | ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ​ ρ 2 ​ 𝑑 ρ \displaystyle\lesssim|f(t+1,\omega)|^{2}\,(t+1)^{2}+\int_{t+1}^{2(t+1)}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(\rho,\omega)||f(\rho,\omega)|}{(1+|t-\rho|)^{1+2\gamma}}\,\rho^{2}\,d\rho 
 
 
 and as before it follows that 
 

 
 
 ∫ ( t + 1 ) ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 + 2 ​ γ ≲ ∫ S ( t + 1 ) | f | 2 ​ 𝑑 S + ∫ ( t + 1 ) ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) | ∂ r f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x ( 1 + | t − r | ) 2 ​ γ \int\limits_{(t+1)\leq|x|\leq 2(t+1)}\frac{|f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2+2\gamma}}\lesssim\int\limits_{S_{(t+1)}}|f|^{2}\,dS+\int\limits_{(t+1)\leq|x|\leq 2(t+1)}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{(1+|t-r|)^{2\gamma}} 
 
 
 Finally, in the region r ≥ 2 ​ ( t + 1 ) r\geq 2(t+1) the estimate ( 9.1 )
would follow from the Hardy type inequality: 
 

 
 (9.2) 
 
 ∫ | x | ≥ ( t + 1 ) | f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x | x | 2 + 2 ​ γ ≤ ∫ | x | ≥ ( t + 1 ) | ∂ r f ⁡ ( x ) | 2 ​ d ​ x | x | 2 ​ γ + ( t + 1 ) − 1 − 2 ​ γ ​ ∫ S ( t + 1 ) | f | 2 ​ 𝑑 S , \int\limits_{|x|\geq(t+1)}\frac{|f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{|x|^{2+2\gamma}}\leq\int\limits_{|x|\geq(t+1)}\frac{|\partial_{r}f(x)|^{2}\,dx}{|x|^{2\gamma}}+(t+1)^{-1-2\gamma}\int\limits_{S_{(t+1)}}|f|^{2}\,dS, 
 
 
 that hold
provided the left hand side is bounded. One can for the proof
assume that f f ha compact support since we can choose a sequence
of compactly supported functions converging to a given function
 f f in the norm defined by the right hand side as long as the norm
in the left of f f is bounded. ( 9.2 ) for compactly
supported smooth functions can be easily seen from integrating the
identity 
 

 
 
 ∂ r ( r 2 ​ f 2 r 1 + 2 ​ γ ) = 2 ​ r 2 r 1 + 2 ​ γ f ⋅ ∂ r f + ( 1 − 2 γ ) r 2 r 2 + 2 ​ γ f 2 , γ ≠ − 1 / 2 \partial_{r}\left(\frac{r^{2}f^{2}}{r^{1+2\gamma}}\right)=\frac{2r^{2}}{r^{1+2\gamma}}f\cdot\partial_{r}f+(1-2\gamma)\frac{r^{2}}{r^{2+2\gamma}}f^{2},\qquad\gamma\neq-1/2 
 
 
 from r = t + 1 r=t+1 to r = ∞ r=\infty and using Cauchy-Schwarz as above.
∎ 
 
 
 
 We now state the global Sobolev inequality, which is
due to S. Klainerman [ K1 ] . 
 
 
 Proposition 9.2 . 
 
 The following inequality holds for an arbitrary smooth
function ϕ \phi . 
 

 
 
 | ϕ ⁡ ( t , x ) | ​ ( 1 + t + | t − r | ) ​ ( 1 + | t − r | ) 1 / 2 ≤ C ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 3 ‖ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( t , ⋅ ) ‖ L 2 . |\phi(t,x)|(1+t+|t-r|)(1+|t-r|)^{1/2}\leq C\sum_{|I|\leq 3}\|Z^{I}\phi(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 10 Decay estimates for the wave equation on a curved space time 

 
 In this section we will derive some basic estimates for the scalar
wave equation on a curved background. The results will require some
weak assumptions on the metric g g , which will be easily verified in
the case of a metric satisfying the reduced Einstein equations. 
 
 
 We consider the reduced
scalar wave equation: 
 

 
 (10.1) 
 
 □ ~ g ​ ϕ = F . \widetilde{\square}_{g}\phi=F. 
 
 
 The following result is a generalization of the lemma in [ L1 ] 
to the variable coefficient case: 
 
 
 Lemma 10.1 . 
 
 Suppose that ϕ \phi satisfies the
reduced scalar wave equation ( 10.1 ) on a curved background
with a metric g g . Suppose that
 H α ​ β = g α ​ β − m α ​ β H^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta} satisfies 
 

 
 (10.2) 
 
 | H | ≤ 1 4 , and | H | L ​ 𝒯 ≤ 1 4 ​ | q | + 1 1 + t + | x | . |H|\leq\frac{1}{4},\qquad\text{and}\qquad|H|_{L{\cal T}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\frac{|q|+1}{1+t+|x|}. 
 
 
 when t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t and 
 

 
 (10.3) 
 
 ∫ 0 ∞ ∥ H ( t , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D t ) d ​ t 1 + t ≤ 1 4 , where D t = { x ∈ ℝ 3 ; t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 t } . \int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\|\,H(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{t})}\frac{dt}{1+t}\leq\frac{1}{4},\qquad\text{where}\qquad D_{t}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3};\,t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t\}. 
 
 
 Then for any t ≥ 0 t\geq 0 and x ∈ ℝ 3 x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3} , 
 

 
 (10.4) 
 
 ( 1 + t + | x | ) ​ | ∂ ϕ ⁡ ( t , x ) | ≤ C ​ sup 0 ≤ τ ≤ t ∑ | I | ≤ 1 ‖ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( τ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ + C ∫ 0 t ( ( 1 + τ ) ∥ F ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) + ∑ | I | ≤ 2 ( 1 + τ ) − 1 ∥ Z I ϕ ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) ) d τ (1+t+|x|)\,|\partial\phi(t,x)|\leq C\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\!\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\!\|Z^{I}\!\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big((1+\tau)\|F(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}+\sum_{|I|\leq 2}(1+\tau)^{-1}\|Z^{I}\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\Big)\,d\tau 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Since by Lemma 7.2 
 

 
 (10.5) 
 
 ( 1 + | t − r | ) ​ | ∂ ϕ | + ( 1 + t + r ) ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | ≤ C ​ ∑ | I | = 1 | Z I ​ ϕ | , r = | x | , (1+|t-r|)|\partial\phi|+(1+t+r)|\bar{\partial}\phi|\leq C\sum_{|I|=1}|Z^{I}\phi|,\qquad r=|x|, 
 
 
 the inequality
( 10.4 ) holds when r < t / 2 + 1 / 2 r<t/2+1/2 or r > 2 ​ t − 1 r>2t-1 .
Furthermore, since 
 

 
 (10.6) 
 
 ( 1 + r ) | ∂ q ϕ | ≤ C | ∂ q ( r ​ ϕ ) | + C ​ | ϕ | , r ≥ 1 (1+r)|\partial_{q}\phi|\leq C|\partial_{q}(r\phi)|+C|\phi|,\qquad r\geq 1 
 
 
 it follows that 
 

 
 (10.7) 
 
 ( 1 + t + r ) ​ | ∂ ϕ | ≤ C ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ ϕ | + C ​ | ∂ q ( r ​ ϕ ) | (1+t+r)|\partial\phi|\leq C\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}\phi|+C|\partial_{q}(r\phi)| 
 
 
 Hence it suffices to prove that
 | ∂ q ( r ​ ϕ ) | |\partial_{q}(r\phi)| is bounded by the right hand side of
( 10.4 ) when t / 2 + 1 / 2 < r < 2 ​ t − 1 t/2+1/2<r<2t-1 . By Lemma
 7.3 
 

 
 (10.8) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q ) ∂ q ( r ϕ ) | ≲ ( 1 + r ​ | H | ℒ𝒯 1 + | q | + | H | ) r − 1 ∑ | I | ≤ 2 | Z I ϕ | + | H | r − 1 | ∂ q ( r ϕ ) | + r | F | \big|(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q})\partial_{q}(r\phi)\big|\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{r\,|H|_{\cal LT}}{1+|q|}+|H|\Big)r^{-1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}|Z^{I}\phi|+|H|\,r^{-1}\,|\partial_{q}(r\phi)|+r|F| 
 
 
 and using the
decay assumptions ( 10.2 ) and ( 10.7 ) we
get 
 

 
 (10.9) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q ) ∂ q ( r ϕ ) | ≲ | H | 1 + t | ∂ q ( r ϕ ) | + ∑ | I | ≤ 2 Z I ϕ | 1 + t + C ( t + 1 ) | F | , when t / 2 + 1 / 2 ≤ r ≤ 2 t − 1 \big|(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q})\partial_{q}(r\phi)\big|\lesssim\frac{|H|}{1+t}|\partial_{q}(r\phi)|+\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\frac{Z^{I}\phi|}{1+t}+C(t+1)|F|,\qquad\text{when}\quad t/2+1/2\leq r\leq 2t-1 
 
 
 Along an integral curve ( t , x ⁡ ( t ) ) (t,x(t)) of the vector field
 ∂ s + H L ¯ ​ L ¯ ( 2 g L ​ L ¯ ) − 1 ∂ q \partial_{s}+H^{\underline{L}\underline{L}}(2g^{L\underline{L}})^{-1}\partial_{q} , contained in the region
 t / 2 + 1 / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t − 1 t/2+1/2\leq|x|\leq 2t-1 , we have the following equation for
 ψ = ∂ q ( r ​ ϕ ) \psi=\partial_{q}(r\phi) : 
 

 
 (10.10) 
 
 | d d ​ t ​ ψ | ≤ h ^ ​ | ψ | + f \Big|\frac{d}{dt}\psi\Big|\leq\hat{h}|\psi|+f 
 
 
 where h ^ = C ​ | H | / ( 1 + t ) \hat{h}=C|H|/(1+t) 
and f = C ​ t ​ | F | + C ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 2 | Z I ​ ϕ | / ( 1 + t ) f=Ct|F|+C\sum_{|I|\leq 2}|Z^{I}\phi|/(1+t) . Hence multiplying
( 10.10 ) with the integrating factor e − H ^ e^{-\hat{H}} , where
 H ^ = ∫ h ^ ​ ( s ) ​ 𝑑 s \hat{H}=\int\hat{h}(s)\,ds we get 
 

 
 (10.11) 
 
 | d d ​ t ​ ( ψ ​ e − H ^ ) | ≤ f ​ e − H ^ . \Big|\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\psi e^{-\hat{H}}\Big)\Big|\leq fe^{-\hat{H}}. 
 
 
 If
we integrate backwards along an integral curve from any point
 ( t , x ) (t,x) in the set t / 2 + 1 / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t − 1 t/2+1/2\leq|x|\leq 2t-1 until the first time
the curve intersects the boundary of the set at ( τ , y ) (\tau,y) ,
 | y | = τ / 2 + 1 / 2 |y|=\tau/2+1/2 or | y | = 2 ​ τ − 1 |y|=2\,\tau-1 , we obtain 
 

 
 
 | ψ ⁡ ( t , x ) | ≤ exp ⁡ ( ∫ τ t ‖ h ^ ​ ( σ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ​ 𝑑 σ ) ​ | ψ ⁡ ( τ , y ) | + ∫ τ t exp ⁡ ( ∫ τ ′ t ‖ h ^ ​ ( σ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ​ 𝑑 σ ) ​ ‖ f ⁡ ( τ ′ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ​ d ​ τ ′ , |\psi(t,x)|\leq\exp\Big({\int_{\tau}^{t}\|\hat{h}(\sigma,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\sigma}\Big)|\psi(\tau,y)|+\int_{\tau}^{t}\exp\Big({\int_{\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\|\hat{h}(\sigma,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\sigma}\Big)\|f(\tau^{\prime},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\tau^{\prime}, 
 
 
 where the L ∞ L^{\infty} norms are taken only over the set t / 1 + 1 / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t − 1 t/1+1/2\leq|x|\leq 2t-1 .
(Note that any integral curve has to intersect either of the two boundaries
 r = t / 2 + 1 / 2 r=t/2+1/2 or r = 2 ​ t − 1 r=2t-1 since the slope of the curve x ⁡ ( t ) x(t) 
has to be close to 1 1 when H L ​ L H_{LL} is small.)
The lemma now follows from taking the supremum over x x in the set
 t / 2 + 1 / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t − 1 t/2+1/2\leq|x|\leq 2t-1 ,
using that on the cones | y | = τ / 2 + 1 / 2 |y|=\tau/2+1/2 or | y | = 2 ​ τ − 1 |y|=2\tau-1 
we have that | ψ | ≤ C ​ r ​ | ∂ q ϕ | + C ​ | ϕ | ≤ C ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ ϕ | |\psi|\leq Cr|\partial_{q}\phi|+C|\phi|\leq C\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}\phi| ,
by ( 10.5 ), and using that by
( 10.3 )
 ∫ 0 t ‖ h ^ ​ ( σ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ​ 𝑑 σ ≤ 1 4 . \int_{0}^{t}\|\hat{h}(\sigma,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\sigma\leq\frac{1}{4}. 
∎ 
 
 
 
 For second order derivatives we have an estimate which gives
a slightly worse decay: 
 
 
 Lemma 10.2 . 
 
 Let ϕ \phi be a solution of the
reduced scalar wave equation on a curved background
with a metric g g . Assume that
 H α ​ β = g α ​ β − m α ​ β H^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta} satisfies 
 

 
 (10.12) 
 
 ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ H | ≤ ε ~ 4 , and ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ H | L ​ L + | H | L ​ 𝒯 ≤ ε ~ 4 ​ | q | + 1 1 + t + | x | . \sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}H|\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{4},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}H|_{LL}+|H|_{L{\cal T}}\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{4}\,\frac{|q|+1}{1+t+|x|}. 
 
 
 when t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t 
for some ε ~ ≤ 1 \tilde{\varepsilon}\leq 1 .
Then, for t ≥ 0 t\geq 0 , x ∈ ℝ 3 x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3} , we have 
 

 
 (10.13) 
 
 OPEN ( 1 + t + | x | ) ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( t , x ) | ≤ C ​ sup 0 ≤ τ ≤ t ( 1 + t 1 + τ ) C ​ ε ~ ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 2 ‖ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( τ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ) + C ∫ 0 t ( 1 + t 1 + τ ) C ​ ε ~ ( ∑ | I | ≤ 1 ( 1 + τ ) ∥ Z I F ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) + ∑ | I | ≤ 3 ( 1 + τ ) − 1 ∥ Z I ϕ ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) ) d τ , (1+t+|x|)\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\,|\partial Z^{I}\phi(t,x)|\leq C\!\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big(\frac{1+t}{1+\tau}\Big)^{C\tilde{\varepsilon}}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\!\|Z^{I}\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\\
+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{1+t}{1+\tau}\Big)^{C\tilde{\varepsilon}}\Big(\sum_{|I|\leq 1}(1+\tau)\|Z^{I}F(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}+\sum_{|I|\leq 3}(1+\tau)^{-1}\|Z^{I}\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\Big)\,d\tau, 
 
 
 where D t = { x ∈ ℝ 3 ; t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 t } D_{t}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3};\,t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t\} . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 First when r < t / 2 r<t/2 or r > t / 2 r>t/2 the lemma
trivially follows from ( 10.5 ) with ϕ \phi replaced by
 Z ​ ϕ Z\phi so it only remains to prove the lemma when t / 2 ≤ r ≤ 2 ​ t t/2\leq r\leq 2t . We have 
 

 
 (10.14) 
 
 □ ~ g ​ Z ​ ϕ = F Z = Z ^ ​ F + ( □ ~ g ​ Z ​ ϕ − Z ^ ​ □ ~ g ​ ϕ ) , \widetilde{\square}_{g}Z\phi=F_{Z}=\hat{Z}F+\big(\widetilde{\square}_{g}Z\phi-\hat{Z}\widetilde{\square}_{g}\phi\big), 
 
 
 where by
( 7.18 ) the additional commutator term can
be estimated by 
 

 
 (10.15) 
 
 | □ ~ g ​ Z ​ ϕ − Z ^ ​ □ ~ g ​ ϕ | ≲ ( | Z ​ H | + | H | 1 + t + | q | + | Z ​ H | L ​ L + | H | L ​ 𝒯 1 + | q | ) ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ Z I ​ ϕ | ≲ ε ~ 1 + t + q ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ Z I ​ ϕ | , \big|\widetilde{\square}_{g}Z\phi-\hat{Z}\widetilde{\square}_{g}\phi|\lesssim\bigg(\frac{|ZH|+|H|}{1+t+|q|}+\frac{|ZH|_{LL}+|H|_{L\cal T}}{1+|q|}\bigg)\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial Z^{I}\phi|\lesssim\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{1+t+q}\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial Z^{I}\phi|, 
 
 
 where we used the decay assumption ( 10.12 ).
Furthermore with the help of ( 10.7 ), applied to Z I ​ ϕ Z^{I}\phi in place
of ϕ \phi , we obtain 
 

 
 (10.16) 
 
 | □ ~ g ​ Z ​ ϕ − Z ^ ​ □ ~ g ​ ϕ | ≲ ε ~ ( 1 + t + | q | ) 2 ​ ( ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ q ( r ​ Z I ​ ϕ ) | + ∑ | I | ≤ 2 | Z I ​ ϕ | ) , \big|\widetilde{\square}_{g}Z\phi-\hat{Z}\widetilde{\square}_{g}\phi|\lesssim\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{(1+t+|q|)^{2}}\Big(\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\big|\partial_{q}(rZ^{I}\phi)\big|+\sum_{|I|\leq 2}|Z^{I}\phi|\Big), 
 
 
 
 
 Hence by ( 10.9 ) applied to ( 10.14 ) in place of
( 10.1 ) we get 
 

 
 (10.17) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q ) ∂ q ( r Z ϕ ) | ≲ ∑ | I | ≤ 3 | Z I ​ ϕ | 1 + t + ε ~ 1 + t ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ q ( r Z I ϕ ) | + t ( | Z F | + | F | ) |(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q})\partial_{q}(rZ\phi)|\lesssim\sum_{|I|\leq 3}\frac{|Z^{I}\phi|}{1+t}+\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{1+t}\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial_{q}(rZ^{I}\phi)|+t(|ZF|+|F|) 
 
 
 when t / 2 + 1 / 2 ≤ r ≤ 2 ​ t − 1 t/2+1/2\leq r\leq 2t-1 . Therefore 
 

 
 (10.18) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q ) ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ q ( r Z I ϕ ) | | ≤ C ​ ε ~ 1 + t ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ q ( r Z I ϕ ) | + C ∑ | I | ≤ 3 | Z I ​ ϕ | 1 + t + C t ( | Z F | + | F | ) \Big|(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q})\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial_{q}(rZ^{I}\phi)|\Big|\leq\frac{C\tilde{\varepsilon}}{1+t}\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial_{q}(rZ^{I}\phi)|+C\sum_{|I|\leq 3}\frac{|Z^{I}\phi|}{1+t}+Ct(|ZF|+|F|) 
 
 
 The desired result follows
multiplying ( 10.17 ) by the factor ( 1 + t ) − C ​ ε ~ (1+t)^{-C\tilde{\varepsilon}} 
and integrating as in the proof of the previous lemma. Along an
integral curve we have the equation 
 

 
 (10.19) 
 
 | d d ​ t ​ ( ψ ​ ( 1 + t ) − C ​ ε ~ ) | ≤ ( 1 + t ) − C ​ ε ~ ​ f , \Big|\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\psi(1+t)^{-C\tilde{\varepsilon}}\Big)\Big|\leq(1+t)^{-C\tilde{\varepsilon}}f, 
 
 
 where 
 

 
 (10.20) 
 
 ψ = ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ q ( Z I ​ ϕ ) | , f = C ⁡ ( 1 + t ) ​ ( | Z ​ F | + | F | ) + C ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 3 | Z I ​ ϕ | 1 + t \psi=\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\partial_{q}(Z^{I}\phi)|,\quad f=C(1+t)(|ZF|+|F|)+C\sum_{|I|\leq 3}\frac{|Z^{I}\phi|}{1+t} 
 
 
 The lemma now follows as in the proof
of Lemma 10.1 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 We observe that similar estimates hold for a system 
 

 
 (10.21) 
 
 □ ~ g ​ ϕ μ ​ ν = F μ ​ ν {\widetilde{\square}}_{g}\phi_{\mu\nu}=F_{\mu\nu} 
 
 
 In particular, in our case, certain components of F μ ​ ν F_{\mu\nu} 
expressed in the null-frame will decay better than others and for
these components we will also get better estimates for
 ϕ μ ​ ν \phi_{\mu\nu} . Since the vector fields L L and L ¯ \underline{L} commute
with contractions of any of the vector fields { L , L ¯ , S 1 , S 2 } \{L,\underline{L},S_{1},S_{2}\} 
proofs of the preceding lemmas imply the following
result: 
 
 
 Corollary 10.3 . 
 
 Let ϕ μ ​ ν \phi_{\mu\nu} be a solution
of reduced wave equation system ( 10.21 ) on a curved background
with a metric g g . Assume that
 H α ​ β = g α ​ β − m α ​ β H^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta} satisfies 
 

 
 (10.22) 
 
 ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ H | ≤ ε ~ 4 , and ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ H | L ​ L + | H | L ​ 𝒯 ≤ ε ~ 4 ​ | q | + 1 1 + t + | x | . \sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}H|\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{4},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}H|_{LL}+|H|_{L{\cal T}}\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{4}\,\frac{|q|+1}{1+t+|x|}. 
 
 
 when t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t , for some ε ~ ≤ 1 \tilde{\varepsilon}\leq 1 and 
 

 
 (10.23) 
 
 ∫ 0 ∞ ‖ H ⁡ ( t , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ ​ ( D t ) ​ d ​ t 1 + t ≤ ε ~ 4 . \int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\|\,H(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{t})}\frac{dt}{1+t}\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{4}. 
 
 
 where D t = { x ∈ ℝ 3 ; t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 t } D_{t}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3};\,t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t\} .
Then for any U , V ∈ { L , L ¯ , S 1 , S 2 } U,V\in\{L,\underline{L},S_{1},S_{2}\} and any t ≥ 0 t\geq 0 , x ∈ ℝ 3 x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3} : 
 

 
 (10.24) 
 
 ( 1 + t + | x | ) \displaystyle(1+t+|x|)\, 
 | ∂ ϕ ⁡ ( t , x ) | U ​ V ≤ C ​ sup 0 ≤ τ ≤ t ∑ | I | ≤ 1 ‖ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( τ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ \displaystyle\,|\partial\phi(t,x)|_{UV}\leq C\!\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\!\|Z^{I}\!\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}} 
 
 
 
 
 + C ∫ 0 t ( ( 1 + τ ) ∥ | F | U ​ V ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) + ∑ | I | ≤ 2 ( 1 + τ ) − 1 ∥ Z I ϕ ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) ) d τ , \displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big((1+\tau)\||F|_{UV}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}+\sum_{|I|\leq 2}(1+\tau)^{-1}\,\|Z^{I}\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\Big)\,d\tau, 
 
 
 (10.25) 
 
 ( 1 + t + | x | ) \displaystyle(1+t+|x|) 
 ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ Z I ​ ϕ | ( t , x ) | ≤ C ​ sup 0 ≤ τ ≤ t ( 1 + t 1 + τ ) C ​ ε ~ ​ ∑ | I | ≤ 2 ‖ Z I ​ ϕ ​ ( τ , ⋅ ) ‖ L ∞ \displaystyle\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\,|\partial Z^{I}\phi|(t,x)|\leq C\!\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big(\frac{1+t}{1+\tau}\Big)^{C\tilde{\varepsilon}}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\!\!\!\|Z^{I}\!\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}} 
 
 
 
 
 + C ∫ 0 t ( 1 + t 1 + τ ) C ​ ε ~ ( ∑ | I | ≤ 1 ∥ r ( ⋅ ) | Z I F | ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) + ∑ | I | ≤ 3 ( 1 + τ ) − 1 ∥ Z I ϕ ( τ , ⋅ ) ∥ L ∞ ​ ( D τ ) ) d τ . \displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{1+t}{1+\tau}\Big)^{C\tilde{\varepsilon}}\Big(\sum_{|I|\leq 1}\|r(\cdot)|Z^{I}F|(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}+\sum_{|I|\leq 3}(1+\tau)^{-1}\,\|Z^{I}\phi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\tau})}\Big)\,d\tau. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By Lemma 7.3 for each component
we have the estimate 
 

 
 (10.26) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q − tr ¯ ​ H + H L ​ L ¯ 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ​ r ) ∂ q ( r ϕ μ ​ ν ) + r ​ F μ ​ ν 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ | ≲ ( 1 + r ​ | H | ℒ𝒯 1 + | q | + | H | ) r − 1 ∑ | I | ≤ 2 | Z I ϕ μ ​ ν | \Big|\Big(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q}-\frac{\overline{\operatorname{tr}}\,H\,+H_{L\underline{L}}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}\,\,r}\Big)\partial_{q}(r\phi_{\mu\nu})+\frac{rF_{\mu\nu}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\Big|\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{r\,|H|_{\cal LT}}{1+|q|}+|H|\Big)r^{-1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}|Z^{I}\phi_{\mu\nu}| 
 
 
 and since ∂ s \partial_{s} and ∂ q \partial_{q} commute with
contractions with the frame vectors L , L ¯ L,\underline{L} we get 
 

 
 (10.27) 
 
 | ( 4 ∂ s − H L ​ L 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ∂ q − tr ¯ ​ H + H L ​ L ¯ 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ ​ r ) ∂ q ( r ϕ U ​ V ) + r ​ F U ​ V 2 ​ g L ​ L ¯ | ≲ ( 1 + r ​ | H | ℒ𝒯 1 + | q | + | H | ) r − 1 ∑ | I | ≤ 2 | Z I ϕ | \Big|\big(4\partial_{s}-\frac{H_{LL}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\partial_{q}-\frac{\overline{\operatorname{tr}}\,H\,+H_{L\underline{L}}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}\,\,r}\Big)\partial_{q}(r\phi_{UV})+\frac{rF_{UV}}{2g^{L\underline{L}}}\Big|\lesssim\Big(1+\frac{r\,|H|_{\cal LT}}{1+|q|}+|H|\Big)r^{-1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}|Z^{I}\phi| 
 
 
 As before it also follows that 
 

 
 (10.28) 
 
 ( 1 + t + | r ) ​ | ∂ ϕ | U ​ V ≲ ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ ϕ | + | ∂ q ( r ​ ϕ ) | U ​ V (1+t+|r)|\partial\phi|_{UV}\lesssim\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|Z^{I}\phi|+|\partial_{q}(r\phi)|_{UV} 
 
 
 The lemma now follows as before.
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 11 Energy estimates for the wave equation on a curved space time 

 
 In this section we derive the energy estimate for a solution
 ϕ \phi of the inhomogeneous wave equation 
 

 
 (11.1) 
 
 □ ~ g ​ ϕ = F \widetilde{\square}_{g}\phi=F 
 
 
 under the following assumptions on the metric
 g α ​ β = m α ​ β + H α ​ β g^{\alpha\beta}=m^{\alpha\beta}+H^{\alpha\beta} : 
 

 
 
 
 ( 1 + | q | ) − 1 ​ | H | L ​ L + | ∂ H | L ​ L + | ​ ∂ ¯ ​ H | ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 , \displaystyle(1+|q|)^{-1}|H|_{LL}+|\partial H|_{LL}+|\overline{\partial}H|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1}, 
 
 
 (11.2) 
 
 
 ( 1 + | q | ) − 1 ​ | H | + | ∂ H | ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 2 ​ ( 1 + | q | ) − 1 2 − γ \displaystyle(1+|q|)^{-1}\,|H|+|\partial H|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+|q|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma} 
 
 
 
 
 Proposition 11.1 . 
 
 Let ϕ \phi be a solution of the wave equation ( 11.1 )
with the metric g g verifying the assumptions ( 11.2 ).
Then for any 0 < γ ≤ 1 / 2 0<\gamma\leq 1/2 , there is an ε 0 \varepsilon_{0} 
such that for ε < ε 0 \varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0} 
 

 
 (11.3) 
 
 ∫ Σ t | ∂ ϕ | 2 + ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ γ ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ≤ 8 ​ ∫ Σ 0 | ∂ ϕ | 2 + C ​ ε ​ ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ t | ∂ ϕ | 2 1 + t + 16 ​ ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ t | F | ​ | ∂ t ϕ | \int_{\Sigma_{t}}|\partial\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{\gamma\,\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2}}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}\leq 8\int_{\Sigma_{0}}|\partial\phi|^{2}+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\frac{|\partial\phi|^{2}}{1+t}+16\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}|F||\partial_{t}\phi| 
 
 
 
 
 
 Remark 11.2 . 
 
 Observe that by the Gronwall inequality the energy estimate of the
above proposition implies t ε t^{\varepsilon} growth of the energy.
For similar estimates, proved under different assumptions, see also
 [ S1 ] , [ A2 ] , [ A3 ] . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The proof of the proposition relies on the energy estimate obtained
in Corollary 8.2 
 

 
 
 ∫ Σ t ( | ∂ t ϕ | 2 + | ∇ ϕ | 2 ) \displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\big(|\partial_{t}\phi|^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2}\big) 
 + ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ γ ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ≤ 4 ∫ Σ 0 ( | ∂ t ϕ | 2 + | ∇ ϕ | 2 ) \displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{\gamma\,\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2}}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}\leq 4\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\big(|\partial_{t}\phi|^{2}+|\nabla\phi|^{2}\big) 
 
 
 
 
 + 8 ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ | ∂ α g α ​ β ∂ β ϕ ∂ t ϕ − 1 2 ∂ t g α ​ β ∂ α ϕ ∂ β ϕ + F ∂ t ϕ | \displaystyle+8\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\big|\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi+F\partial_{t}\phi\big| 
 
 
 
 
 + 2 ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ γ ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ | ( g α ​ β − m α ​ β ) ∂ α ϕ ∂ β ϕ + 2 ( g L ¯ ​ β − m L ¯ ​ β ) ∂ β ϕ ∂ t ϕ | \displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{\gamma}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}\big|(g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi+2(g^{\underline{L}\beta}-m^{\underline{L}\beta})\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi\big| 
 
 
 We start by decomposing the terms on the right hand side with
respect to the null frame. 
 

 
 
 | ∂ α g α ​ β ​ ∂ β ϕ ​ ∂ t ϕ | ≤ ( | H | ​ | ∂ H | + | ( ∂ H ) L ​ L | + | ∂ ¯ ​ H | ) ​ | ∂ ϕ | 2 + | ∂ H ​ ‖ ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ ‖ ​ ∂ ϕ | |\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi|\leq\big(|H|\,|\partial H|+|(\partial H)_{LL}|+|\bar{\partial}H|\big)\,|\partial\phi|^{2}+|\partial H|\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|\,|\partial\phi| 
 
 
 Similarly, 
 

 
 
 | ∂ t g α ​ β ​ ∂ α ϕ ​ ∂ β ϕ | ≤ ( | g − m | ​ | ∂ g | + | ( ∂ g ) L ​ L | + | ∂ ¯ ​ g | ) ​ | ∂ ϕ | 2 + | ∂ g ​ ‖ ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ ‖ ​ ∂ ϕ | |\partial_{t}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi|\leq\big(|g-m|\,|\partial g|+|(\partial g)_{LL}|+|\bar{\partial}g|\big)\,|\partial\phi|^{2}+|\partial g|\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|\,|\partial\phi| 
 
 
 Therefore, using the assumptions ( 11.2 ) on the metric
 g g , we obtain that 
 

 
 (11.4) 
 
 | ∂ α g α ​ β ​ ∂ β ϕ ​ ∂ t ϕ − 1 2 ​ ∂ t g α ​ β ​ ∂ α ϕ ​ ∂ β ϕ | ≲ ε 1 + t ​ | ∂ ϕ | 2 + ε ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 |\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi|\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon}{1+t}|\partial\phi|^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2} 
 
 
 Decomposing the remaining terms we infer that 
 

 
 
 | ( g α ​ β − m α ​ β ) ​ ∂ α ϕ ​ ∂ β ϕ | ≤ | H L ​ L | | ∂ ϕ | 2 + | H ​ ‖ ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ ‖ ​ ∂ ϕ | |(g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi|\leq|H_{LL}|\,|\partial\phi|^{2}+|H||\bar{\partial}\phi|\,|\partial\phi| 
 
 
 Similarly, 
 

 
 
 | ( g α ​ β − m α ​ β ) ​ L α ​ ∂ β ϕ ​ ∂ t ϕ | ≤ | H L ​ L | | ∂ ϕ | 2 + | H ​ ‖ ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ ‖ ​ ∂ ϕ | |(g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta})L_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi|\leq|H_{LL}|\,|\partial\phi|^{2}+|H|\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|\,|\partial\phi| 
 
 
 Once again, using the assumptions ( 11.2 ), we have 
 

 
 (11.5) 
 
 | 2 ​ ( g α ​ β − m α ​ β ) ​ L α ​ ∂ β ϕ ​ ∂ t ϕ + ( g α ​ β − m α ​ β ) ​ ∂ α ϕ ​ ∂ β ϕ | ≲ ε ​ 1 + | q | 1 + t ​ | ∂ ϕ | 2 + ε ( 1 + | q | ) 2 ​ γ ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 |2(g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta})L_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}\phi\partial_{t}\phi+(g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\beta}\phi|\lesssim\varepsilon\frac{1+|q|}{1+t}|\partial\phi|^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{(1+|q|)^{2\gamma}}|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2} 
 
 
 Thus 
 

 
 
 ∫ Σ t | ∂ ϕ | 2 + ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ γ ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ≤ 4 ​ ∫ Σ 0 | ∂ ϕ | 2 + C ​ ε ​ ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ ( | ∂ ϕ | 2 1 + t + | ∂ ¯ ​ ϕ | 2 ( 1 + | q | ) 1 + 2 ​ γ ) + 8 ​ ∫ 0 t ∫ Σ τ | F | ​ | ∂ t ϕ | \int_{\Sigma_{t}}|\partial\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\frac{\gamma\,\,|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2}}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}\leq 4\int_{\Sigma_{0}}|\partial\phi|^{2}+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\Big(\frac{|\partial\phi|^{2}}{1+t}+\frac{|\bar{\partial}\phi|^{2}}{(1+|q|)^{1+2\gamma}}\Big)+8\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|F|\,|\partial_{t}\phi| 
 
 
 and the desired estimate follows if we take ε \varepsilon so small that
 C ​ ε < γ / 2 C\varepsilon<\gamma/2 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 12 Estimates from the wave coordinate condition 

 
 In previous sections we have shown that one only needs to control
certain components of the metric in order to establish decay
estimates for solutions of the reduced wave equation. In this section we
will see that the wave coordinate condition allows one to estimate
precisely those components in terms of tangential derivatives or
higher order terms with better decay better. Recall that the wave coordinate condition
can be written in the form 
 

 
 (12.1) 
 
 ∂ μ ( g μ ​ ν ​ | det g | ) = 0 \partial_{\mu}\Big(g^{\mu\nu}\sqrt{|\det{g}|}\Big)=0 
 
 
 We have the following decomposition 
 

 
 
 g μ ​ ν ​ | det g | = ( m μ ​ ν + H μ ​ ν ) ​ ( 1 − 1 2 ​ tr ​ H + O ⁡ ( H 2 ) ) g^{\mu\nu}\sqrt{|\det{g}|}=\big(m^{\mu\nu}+H^{\mu\nu}\big)\big(1-\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}H+O(H^{2})\big) 
 
 
 where
 H α ​ β = g α ​ β − m α ​ β H^{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta} ,
 h α ​ β = g α ​ β − m α ​ β h_{\alpha\beta}=g_{\alpha\beta}-m_{\alpha\beta} . Recall also
that g α ​ β g^{\alpha\beta} is the inverse of g α ​ β g_{\alpha\beta} and
 H α ​ β = − m μ ​ α ​ m ν ​ β ​ h μ ​ ν + O ⁡ ( h 2 ) H^{\alpha\beta}=-m^{\mu\alpha}m^{\nu\beta}h_{\mu\nu}+O(h^{2}) .
Therefore we obtain the following expression for the wave coordinate
condition: 
 

 
 (12.2) 
 
 ∂ μ ( H μ ​ ν − 1 2 ​ m μ ​ ν ​ tr ⁡ H + O μ ​ ν ​ ( H 2 ) ) = 0 \partial_{\mu}\Big(H^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}m^{\mu\nu}\operatorname{tr}\,H+O^{\mu\nu}(H^{2})\Big)=0 
 
 
 Using that we can express the divergence in terms of the null frame 
 

 
 (12.3) 
 
 ∂ μ F μ = L μ ​ ∂ q F μ − L ¯ μ ​ ∂ s F μ + A μ ​ ∂ A F μ \partial_{\mu}F^{\mu}=L_{\mu}\partial_{q}F^{\mu}-\underline{L}_{\mu}\partial_{s}F^{\mu}+A_{\mu}\partial_{A}F^{\mu} 
 
 
 we obtain: 
 
 
 Lemma 12.1 . 
 
 Assume that
 | H | ≤ 1 / 4 |H|\leq 1/4 . Then 
 

 
 (12.4) 
 
 | ∂ H | L ​ 𝒯 ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ H | + | H | | ∂ H | , | ∂ tr ¯ ​ H | ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ H | + | H | ​ | ∂ H | |\partial H|_{L\cal T}\lesssim|\overline{\partial}H|+|H|\,|\partial H|,\qquad\quad|\partial\,\overline{\operatorname{tr}}\,H|\lesssim|\bar{\partial}H|+|H|\,|\partial H| 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 It follows from ( 12.2 ) and
( 12.3 ) that 
 

 
 (12.5) 
 
 | L μ ​ ∂ ( H μ ​ ν − 1 2 ​ m μ ​ ν ​ tr ⁡ H ) | ≤ | ∂ ¯ ​ H | + | H | ​ | ∂ H | \big|L_{\mu}\partial\big(H^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}m^{\mu\nu}\operatorname{tr}\,H\big)\big|\leq|\bar{\partial}H|+|H||\partial H| 
 
 
 Contracting with T ν T_{\nu} and using that m T ​ L = 0 m_{TL}=0 gives the
first inequality and contracting with L ¯ μ \underline{L}_{\mu} 
and using that m L ¯ ​ L = − 2 m_{\underline{L}L}=-2 gives the second since 
 

 
 (12.6) 
 
 H L ​ L ¯ + tr ⁡ H = tr ¯ ​ H H_{L\underline{L}}+\operatorname{tr}\,H=\overline{\operatorname{tr}}\,H 
 
 
 ∎ 
 
 
 
 We now compute the commutators of the wave coordinate condition
with the vector fields Z Z . 
 
 
 Lemma 12.2 . 
 
 Let Z Z be one of the Minkowski Killing or conformally Killing
vector fields and let tensor H H satisfy the wave coordinate
condition. Then the estimate 
 

 
 
 | ∂ H J | L ​ 𝒯 ≲ ∑ | J | ≤ | I | | ∂ ¯ ​ Z J ​ H | + ∑ I 1 + … + I k = I , k ≥ 2 | Z I k ​ H | + ⋯ | Z I 2 ​ H | | ∂ Z I 1 ​ H | \displaystyle\big|\partial H^{J}\big|_{L\cal T}\lesssim\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}|\bar{\partial}Z^{J}H|+\sum_{I_{1}+...+I_{k}=I,\,k\geq 2}|Z^{I_{k}}H|\cdot\cdot\cdot|Z^{I_{2}}H|\,|\partial Z^{I_{1}}H| 
 
 
 holds true for the expression 
 

 
 (12.7) 
 
 H μ ​ ν J = Z J ​ H ~ μ ​ ν + ∑ | J | < | I | c J ​ μ I ​ γ ​ Z J ​ H ~ γ ​ ν , where H ~ μ ​ ν = H μ ​ ν − 1 2 ​ m μ ​ ν ​ tr ​ H H_{\mu\nu}^{J}=Z^{J}\widetilde{H}_{\mu\nu}+\!\!\!\!\sum_{|J|<|I|}c_{J\,\mu}^{I\,\,\,\,\gamma}Z^{J}\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\nu},\qquad{\text{where}}\qquad\widetilde{H}_{\mu\nu}=H_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}m_{\mu\nu}\text{tr}H 
 
 
 with some constant tensors c J I ​ γ ​ μ c_{J}^{I\,\gamma\mu} such
that c J ​ L I ​ L ¯ = 0 c_{JL}^{I\,\,\,\underline{L}}=0 if | J | = | I | − 1 |J|=|I|-1 . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The wave coordinate condition ( 12.1 ) can be written
in the form 
 

 
 
 ∂ μ ( G ~ μ ​ ν ) = 0 , where G ~ μ ​ ν = ( m μ ​ ν + H μ ​ ν ) ​ | det g | . \partial_{\mu}\big(\widetilde{G}^{\mu\nu}\big)=0,\qquad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{G}^{\mu\nu}=(m^{\mu\nu}+H^{\mu\nu})\sqrt{|\det g|}. 
 
 
 Let Z Z be one of the Minkowski Killing or conformally Killing
vector fields. Then for any vector field F F we have that 
 

 
 
 Z I ∂ α F α = ∂ α ( Z I F α + ∑ | J | < | I | c J ​ γ I ​ α Z J F γ ) = ∂ α ( ∑ | J ≤ | I | c J ​ γ I ​ α Z J F γ ) , Z^{I}\partial_{\alpha}F^{\alpha}=\partial_{\alpha}\Big(Z^{I}F^{\alpha}+\sum_{|J|<|I|}c_{J\,\,\gamma}^{\,I\,\,\,\alpha}Z^{J}F^{\gamma}\Big)=\partial_{\alpha}\Big(\sum_{|J\leq|I|}c_{J\,\gamma}^{\,I\,\,\,\alpha}Z^{J}F^{\gamma}\Big), 
 
 
 where c J ​ γ α c_{J\,\gamma}^{\,\,\,\alpha} are constants such that 
 

 
 
 c J ​ γ I ​ α = δ γ α , if | J | = | I | and c J ​ L I ​ L ¯ = 0 , if | J | = | I | − 1 c_{J\,\gamma}^{\,I\,\,\,\alpha}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\,\gamma},\quad\text{if}\quad|J|=|I|\qquad\text{and}\qquad c_{J\,L}^{\,I\,\,\,\underline{L}}=0,\quad\text{if}\quad|J|=|I|-1 
 
 
 The last identity is a consequence of the relation between c J ​ α I ​ γ c_{J\,\alpha}^{\,I\,\,\,\gamma} and the commutator constants
 c α ​ β = [ ∂ α , Z ] β c_{\alpha\beta}=[\partial_{\alpha},Z]_{\beta} for which we have established that
 c L ​ L = 0 c_{LL}=0 . It therefore follows that 
 

 
 
 ∂ μ ( ∑ | J | ≤ | I | c J I ​ μ ​ γ ​ Z J ​ G ~ γ ​ ν ) = 0 . \partial_{\mu}\big(\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}c_{J}^{\,I\,\,\,\mu\gamma}Z^{J}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}\big)=0. 
 
 
 Decomposing relative to the null frame ( L , L ¯ , S 1 , S 2 ) (L,\underline{L},S_{1},S_{2}) we obtain 
 

 
 
 ∂ q ( ∑ | J | ≤ | I | c J I ​ L ¯ ​ γ ​ Z J ​ G ~ γ ​ ν ) = ∂ s ( ∑ | J | ≤ | I | c J I ​ L ​ γ ​ Z J ​ G ~ γ ​ ν ) − A μ ​ ∂ ¯ A ​ ( ∑ | J | ≤ | I | c J I ​ μ ​ γ ​ Z J ​ G ~ γ ​ ν ) . \partial_{q}\big(\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}c_{J}^{\,I\,\,\,\underline{L}\,\gamma}Z^{J}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}\big)=\partial_{s}\Big(\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}c_{J}^{\,I\,\,\,L\gamma}Z^{J}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}\Big)-A_{\mu}\bar{\partial}_{A}\big(\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}c_{J}^{\,I\,\,\,\mu\gamma}Z^{J}\widetilde{G}_{\,\gamma\nu}\big). 
 
 
 We now contract the above identity with one of the tangential
vector fields T ν T^{\nu} , T ∈ { L , S 1 , S 2 } T\in\{L,S_{1},S_{2}\} to obtain 
 

 
 
 | L γ ​ T ν ​ ∂ q Z I ​ G ~ γ ​ ν + ∑ | J | < | I | c J I ​ L ¯ ​ γ ​ T ν ​ ∂ q Z J ​ G ~ γ ​ ν | ≲ ∑ | J | ≤ | I | | ∂ ¯ ​ Z I ​ G ~ | \Big|L^{\gamma}\,T^{\nu}\partial_{q}Z^{I}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}+\!\!\!\!\sum_{|J|<|I|}c_{J}^{\,I\,\,\,\underline{L}\gamma}T^{\nu}\,\partial_{q}Z^{J}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}\Big|\lesssim\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}\big|\bar{\partial}Z^{I}\widetilde{G}\big| 
 
 
 We examine the expression 
 

 
 
 L γ ​ T ν ​ Z J ​ ∂ q G ~ γ ​ ν = L γ ​ T ν ​ ∂ q Z J ​ ( ( m γ ​ ν + H γ ​ ν ) ​ | det g | ) = ∑ J 1 + J 2 = J L γ ​ T ν ​ ∂ q ( ( Z J 1 ​ H γ ​ ν ) ​ Z J 2 ​ | det g | ) L^{\gamma}\,T^{\nu}Z^{J}\partial_{q}\widetilde{G}_{\gamma\nu}=L^{\gamma}\,T^{\nu}\partial_{q}Z^{J}\bigg((m_{\gamma\nu}+H_{\gamma\nu})\sqrt{|\det g|}\bigg)=\sum_{J_{1}+J_{2}=J}L^{\gamma}\,T^{\nu}\partial_{q}\bigg((Z^{J_{1}}H_{\gamma\nu})Z^{J_{2}}\sqrt{|\det g|}\bigg) 
 
 
 since m L ​ T = L γ ​ T ν ​ m γ ​ ν = 0 m_{LT}=L^{\gamma}\,T^{\nu}m_{\gamma\nu}=0 . The desired
estimate now follows from the identity | det g | = 1 + f ⁡ ( H ) \sqrt{|\det g|}=1+f(H) ,
which holds with a smooth function f ⁡ ( H ) f(H) such that
 f ( H ) = − tr H / 2 + O ( H 2 ) f(H)=-\text{tr}H/2+O(H^{2}) 
∎ 
 
 
 
 We now summarize the results of this section in the following 
 
 
 Lemma 12.3 . 
 
 For a tensor H H obeying the wave coordinate condition 
 

 
 (12.8) 
 
 | ∂ H | L ​ T ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ H | + | H | ​ | ∂ H | , |\partial H|_{LT}\lesssim|\bar{\partial}H|+|H|\,|\partial H|, 
 
 
 and 
 

 
 (12.9) 
 
 | ∂ Z ​ H | L ​ L ≲ | ∂ H | L ​ 𝒯 + ∑ | I | ≤ 1 | ∂ ¯ ​ Z I ​ H | + ∑ | I | + | J | ≤ 1 | Z I ​ H | ​ | ∂ Z J ​ H | . |\partial ZH|_{LL}\lesssim|\partial H|_{L\cal T}+\sum_{|I|\leq 1}|\overline{\partial}Z^{I}H|+\sum_{|I|+|J|\leq 1}|Z^{I}H|\,|\partial Z^{J}H|. 
 
 
 In general, 
 

 
 (12.10) 
 
 | ∂ Z I ​ H | L ​ 𝒯 ≲ ∑ | J | ≤ | I | | ∂ ¯ ​ Z J ​ H | + ∑ | J | ≤ | I | − 1 | ∂ Z J ​ H | + ∑ | I 1 | + … + | I m | ≤ | I | , m ≥ 2 | Z I m ​ H | + ⋯ | Z I 2 ​ H | | ∂ Z I 1 ​ H | , |\partial Z^{I}H|_{L\cal T}\lesssim\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}|\overline{\partial}Z^{J}H|+\!\!\!\!\sum_{|J|\leq|I|-1}\!\!\!|\partial Z^{J}H|\,\,+\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\,\,\,\,|I_{1}|+...+|I_{m}|\leq|I|,\,m\geq 2}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|Z^{I_{m}}H|\!\cdot\cdot\cdot|Z^{I_{2}}H||\partial Z^{I_{1}}H|, 
 
 
 and 
 

 
 (12.11) 
 
 | ∂ Z I ​ H | L ​ L ≲ ∑ | J | ≤ | I | | ∂ ¯ ​ Z J ​ H | + ∑ | J | ≤ | I | − 1 | ∂ Z J ​ H | L ​ 𝒯 + ∑ | K | ≤ | I | − 2 | ∂ Z J ​ H | + ∑ | I 1 | + … + | I m | ≤ | I | , m ≥ 2 | Z I m H | ⋯ | Z I 2 H | | ∂ Z I 1 H | . |\partial Z^{I}H|_{LL}\lesssim\sum_{|J|\leq|I|}|\overline{\partial}Z^{J}H|+\sum_{|J|\leq|I|-1}|\partial Z^{J}H|_{L\cal T}+\sum_{|K|\leq|I|-2}|\partial Z^{J}H|\\
+\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{|I_{1}|+...+|I_{m}|\leq|I|,\,m\geq 2}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|Z^{I_{m}}H|\!\cdot\cdot\cdot|Z^{I_{2}}H||\partial Z^{I_{1}}H|. 
 
 
 The same estimates also hold for H H replaced by h h . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 This follows directly by the previous lemma with the help of
the identities m L ​ T = 0 m_{LT}=0 and c J ​ L I ​ L ¯ = 0 c_{J\,L}^{I\,\,\,\underline{L}}=0 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 13 Estimates for the inhomogeneous terms 

 
 In this section we will show that the inhomogeneous terms of the
reduced Einstein equations can be estimated in terms of tangential
derivatives, for which we have better decay estimates, or tangential
components which in turn can be expressed, using the wave coordinate
condition, in terms of tangential derivatives and lower order terms.
Recall that according to Lemma 3.2 the symmetric
two tensor h μ ​ ν = g μ ​ ν − m μ ​ ν h_{\mu\nu}=g_{\mu\nu}-m_{\mu\nu} verifies the reduced
Einstein equations of the form: 
 

 
 
 
 □ ~ g ​ h μ ​ ν = F μ ​ ν ​ ( h ) ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) , \displaystyle\widetilde{\square}_{g}h_{\mu\nu}=F_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h), 
 
 
 (13.1) 
 
 
 F μ ​ ν ​ ( h ) ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) = P ⁡ ( ∂ μ h , ∂ ν h ) + Q μ ​ ν ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) + G μ ​ ν ​ ( h ) ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) , \displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h)=P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h)+Q_{\mu\nu}(\partial h,\partial h)+G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h), 
 
 
 (13.2) 
 
 
 P ⁡ ( ∂ μ k , ∂ ν p ) = 1 4 ​ ∂ μ tr ​ k ​ ∂ ν tr ​ p − 1 2 ​ ∂ μ k α ​ β ​ ∂ ν p α ​ β , \displaystyle P(\partial_{\mu}k,\partial_{\nu}p)=\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\mu}\text{tr}k\,\partial_{\nu}\text{tr}p-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}k^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\nu}p_{\alpha\beta}, 
 
 
 Here Q ​ μ ​ ν Q{\mu\nu} are linear combinations of the null-forms and
 G μ ​ ν ​ ( h ) ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) G_{\mu\nu}(h)(\partial h,\partial h) is a quadratic form in ∂ h \partial h with
coefficients that are smooth functions of h h and vanishing at
 h = 0 h=0 . 
 
 
 Lemma 13.1 . 
 
 The quadratic form P P satisfies the following pointwise estimate: 
 

 
 (13.3) 
 
 
 | P ⁡ ( ∂ p , ∂ k ) | 𝒯𝒰 ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ p | ​ | ∂ k | + | ∂ p | ​ | ∂ ¯ ​ k | , \displaystyle|P(\partial p,\partial k)|_{\cal TU}\lesssim|\bar{\partial}p\,|\,|\partial k|+|\partial p\,|\,|\bar{\partial}k|, 
 
 
 (13.4) 
 
 
 | P ⁡ ( ∂ p , ∂ k ) | ≲ | ∂ p | 𝒯𝒰 ​ | ∂ k | 𝒯𝒰 + | ∂ p | L ​ L ​ | ∂ k | + | ​ ∂ p | | ​ ∂ k | L ​ L \displaystyle|P(\partial p,\partial k)|\lesssim|\partial p\,|_{\cal TU}|\partial k|_{\cal TU}+|\partial p\,|_{LL}|\partial k|+|\partial p\,|\,|\partial k|_{LL} 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The first part of the statement follows trivially from ( 13.2 ).
To prove ( 13.4 ) we use ( 5.9 ) applied to
 R μ ​ ∂ μ p R^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}p in place of p p and S ν ​ ∂ ν k S^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}k in place of
 k k , for any vector fields T T and S S , to obtain 
 

 
 (13.5) 
 
 | T μ ​ S ν ​ P ​ ( ∂ μ p , ∂ ν k ) | ≲ | T μ ​ ∂ μ p | 𝒯𝒰 ​ | S ν ​ ∂ ν k | 𝒯𝒰 + | ​ T μ ​ ∂ μ p | L ​ L | ​ S ν ​ ∂ ν k ​ | + | T μ ​ ∂ μ p | | ​ S ν ​ ∂ ν k | L ​ L |T^{\mu}S^{\nu}\,P(\partial_{\mu}p,\partial_{\nu}k)|\lesssim|T^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}p\,|_{\cal TU}|S^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}k|_{\cal TU}+|T^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}p\,|_{LL}|S^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}k|+|T^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}p\,|\,|S^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}k|_{LL} 
 
 
 which proves the
lemma.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Using the additional estimates
on the h L ​ L h_{LL} component, derived in Lemma 12.3 under the
assumption that the wave coordinate condition holds, we obtain the
following: 
 
 
 Corollary 13.2 . 
 
 Under the additional assumption that h h satisfies the wave
coordinate condition ( 3.4 ), the quadratic form P P 
obeys the estimate 
 

 
 (13.6) 
 
 
 | P ⁡ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) | 𝒯𝒰 ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ h | ​ | ∂ h | , \displaystyle|P(\partial h,\partial h)|_{\cal TU}\lesssim|\bar{\partial}h|\,|\partial h|, 
 
 
 (13.7) 
 
 
 | P ⁡ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) | ≲ | ∂ h | 𝒯𝒰 2 + | ∂ ¯ ​ h | ​ | ∂ h | + | h | ​ | ∂ h | 2 \displaystyle|P(\partial h,\partial h)|\lesssim|\partial h|^{2}_{\cal TU}+|\bar{\partial}h|\,|\partial h|+|h|\,|\partial h|^{2} 
 
 
 Moreover, 
 

 
 
 | Z I ​ P ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) | \displaystyle|Z^{I}P(\partial h,\partial h)| 
 ≲ ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | ( | ∂ Z J ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 | ​ ∂ Z K ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 + | ∂ ¯ ​ Z J ​ h | ​ | ∂ Z K ​ h | ) + ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | − 1 | ∂ Z J ​ h | L ​ 𝒯 ​ | ∂ Z K ​ h | \displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|}\big(|\partial Z^{J}h|_{\cal TU}\,|\partial Z^{K}h|_{\cal TU}+|\bar{\partial}Z^{J}h|\,|\partial Z^{K}h|\big)+\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|-1}|\partial Z^{J}h|_{L\cal T}|\partial Z^{K}h| 
 
 
 
 
 + ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | − 2 | ∂ Z J h | | ∂ Z K h | + ∑ | J 1 | + … + | J m | ≤ | I | , m ≥ 3 | Z J m h | ⋯ | Z J 3 h | | ∂ Z J 2 h | | ∂ Z J 1 h | \displaystyle+\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|-2}|\partial Z^{J}h|\,|\partial Z^{K}h|+\sum_{|J_{1}|+...+|J_{m}|\leq|I|,\,m\geq 3}|Z^{J_{m}}h|\!\cdot\cdot\cdot|Z^{J_{3}}h|\,|\partial Z^{J_{2}}h||\partial Z^{J_{1}}h| 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The inequality ( 13.6 ) follows directly
from ( 13.3 ). To prove ( 13.7 )
we use ( 13.4 ) and
that by the wave coordinate condition
 | ∂ h | L ​ L ≲ | ∂ ¯ ​ h | + | h | ​ | ∂ h | |\partial h|_{LL}\lesssim|\bar{\partial}h|+|h|\,|\partial h| . 
 
 
 We now note that Z I ​ P ​ ( ∂ μ h , ∂ ν h ) Z^{I}P(\partial_{\mu}h,\partial_{\nu}h) is a sum of terms of the
form P ⁡ ( ∂ α Z J ​ h , ∂ β Z K ​ h ) P(\partial_{\alpha}Z^{J}h,\partial_{\beta}Z^{K}h) for some α \alpha , β \beta and
 | J | + | K | ≤ I |J|+|K|\leq I : 
 

 
 
 | Z I ​ P ​ ( ∂ h , ∂ h ) | ≤ C ​ ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | | P ⁡ ( ∂ Z J ​ h , ∂ Z K ​ h ) | |Z^{I}P(\partial h,\partial h)|\leq C\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|}|P(\partial Z^{J}h,\partial Z^{K}h)| 
 
 
 It follows from Lemma 5.4 and Lemma 12.3 that 
 

 
 (13.8) 
 
 ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | | P ⁡ ( ∂ Z J ​ h , ∂ Z K ​ h ) | ≲ ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | | ∂ Z J ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 ​ | ∂ Z K ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 + | ​ ∂ Z J ​ h | L ​ L ​ | ∂ Z K ​ h | ≲ ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | | ∂ ¯ ​ Z J ​ h | ​ | Z K ​ h | + | ∂ Z J ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 | ​ ∂ Z K ​ h | 𝒯𝒰 + ∑ | J | + | K | ≤ | I | ( ∑ | J ′ | ≤ | J | − 1 | ∂ Z J ′ h | ℒ𝒯 + ∑ | J ′′ | ≤ | J | − 2 | ∂ Z J ′′ h | + ∑ | J 1 | + … + | J m | ≤ | J | , m ≥ 2 | Z J m h | ⋯ | Z J 2 h | | ∂ Z J 1 h | ) | ∂ Z K h | \!\!\!\!\!\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|}\!\!\!\!\!|P(\partial Z^{J}h,\partial Z^{K}h)|\lesssim\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{|J|+|K|\leq|I|}\!\!\!\!\!|\partial Z^{J}h|_{\cal TU}|\partial Z^{K}h|_{\cal TU}+|\partial Z^{J}h|_{LL}\,|\partial Z^{K}h|\\

 which proves the lemma.
∎

 14 The decay estimates for Einstein’s
equations 

 
 In this section we will
establish the improved decay estimates for Einstein’s equations. Our strategy is to
use the weak decay estimates, obtained from the assumed energy
bounds, to prove sharper decay estimates and then to recover
the energy bounds in the next section. 
 
 
 Theorem 14.1 . 
 
 Suppose that for some 0 < γ ≤ 1 / 2 0<\gamma\leq 1/2 
 

 
 (14.1) 
 
 
 | ∂ Z I h | ≤ C ε ( 1 + t + | q | ) − 1 / 2 − γ ( 1 + | q | ) − 1 / 2 − γ , | I | ≤ N / 2 + 4 , \displaystyle|\partial Z^{I}h|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t+|q|)^{-1/2-\gamma}(1+|q|)^{-1/2-\gamma},\qquad|I|\leq N/2+4, 
 
 
 (14.2) 
 
 
 | Z I ​ h | ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 , q = 1 , | I | ≤ N / 2 + 4 \displaystyle|Z^{I}h|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1},\qquad q=1,\qquad|I|\leq N/2+4 
 
 
 hold for 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T .
Then for 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T we have 
 

 
 (14.3) 
 
 
 | Z I h | ≤ C ε ( 1 + t + | q | ) − 1 / 2 − γ ( 1 + | q | ) 1 / 2 − γ , | I | ≤ N / 2 + 4 , \displaystyle|Z^{I}h|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t+|q|)^{-1/2-\gamma}(1+|q|)^{1/2-\gamma},\qquad|I|\leq N/2+4, 
 
 
 (14.4) 
 
 
 | ∂ ¯ Z I h | ≤ C ε ( 1 + t + | q | ) − 3 / 2 − γ ( 1 + | q | ) 1 / 2 − γ , | I | ≤ N / 2 + 3 . \displaystyle|\overline{\partial}Z^{I}h|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t+|q|)^{-3/2-\gamma}(1+|q|)^{1/2-\gamma},\qquad|I|\leq N/2+3. 
 
 
 Assume also that
 h h satisfies the wave coordinate condition. Then
for 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T we have 
 

 
 (14.5) 
 
 | ∂ h | L ​ 𝒯 + | ​ ∂ Z ​ h | L ​ L ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 − 2 ​ γ , and | h | L ​ 𝒯 + | ​ Z ​ h | L ​ L ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 ​ ( 1 + | q | ) . |\partial h|_{L\cal T}+|\partial Zh|_{LL}\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1-2\gamma},\qquad\text{and}\qquad|h|_{L\cal T}+|Zh|_{LL}\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1}(1+|q|). 
 
 
 Furthermore if in addition h h satisfies Einstein’s equations then
for ε \varepsilon sufficiently small and 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T 
we also have 
 

 
 (14.6) 
 
 
 | ∂ h | 𝒯𝒰 ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 , | h | 𝒯𝒰 ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 ​ ( 1 + | q | ) , \displaystyle|\partial h|_{\cal TU}\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1},\qquad|h|_{\cal TU}\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1}(1+|q|), 
 
 
 (14.7) 
 
 
 | ∂ h | ≤ C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 ​ ln ⁡ ( 2 + t ) . \displaystyle|\partial h|\leq C\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1}\ln(2+t). 
 
 
 In general, there are constants M k M_{k} , C k C_{k} and
 ε k > 0 \varepsilon_{k}>0 such that if ε ≤ ε k \varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{k} 
then for | I | = k ≤ N / 2 + 2 |I|=k\leq N/2+2 
 

 
 (14.8) 
 
 | ∂ Z I ​ h | ≤ C k ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 + M k ​ ε , and | Z I ​ h | ≤ C k ​ ε ​ ( 1 + t ) − 1 + M k ​ ε ​ ( 1 + | q | ) |\partial Z^{I}h|\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1+M_{k}\varepsilon},\qquad\text{and}\qquad|Z^{I}h|\leq C_{k}\varepsilon(1+t)^{-1+M_{k}\varepsilon}(1+|q|) 
 
 
 
 
 
 Remark 14.2 . 
 
 We remind the reader that, as stated in the Remark 2.4 ,
our estimates make no distinction between the tensors h h and
 H = − h + O ⁡ ( h 2 ) H=-h+O(h^{2}) . In particular, one can directly verify
that the conclusions of the theorem also hold for the tensor H H . 
 
 
 
 First we note that all the estimates
( 14.3 )-( 14.8 ) trivially follow
from the assumptions ( 14.1 )-( 14.2 )
away from the light cone, thus the theorem is only useful in the
region t / 2 ≤ | x | ≤ 2 ​ t t/2\leq|x|\leq 2t . The estimate
( 14.3 ) follows from integrating
( 14.1 ) from q = 1 q=1 , where ( 14.2 ) hold.
Similarly the second parts of ( 14.5 ),
( 14.6 ) and ( 14.8 ) follow from
integrating the first and using ( 14.2 ). It follows
from ( 14.3 ) and Lemma 7.2 that we have
the better estimate ( 14.4 ) for the derivatives tangential
to the outgoing Minkowski cones.
The inequalities ( 14.5 )-( 14.8 ) for tangential
derivatives certainly follow from ( 14.4 ), so it only
remains to prove these estimates for a derivative transversal to
the light cone. 
 
 
 The missing improved estimates for a ( ∂ t − ∂ r ) (\partial_{t}-\partial_{r}) derivative transversal to the
light cones will be obtained, in the case of ( 14.5 ),
from the wave coordinate condition, see section 12 , and for
( 14.6 )-( 14.8 ),
from integrating the reduced Einstein wave equations, see section
 10 .
The estimates from the
wave coordinate condition are easily obtained. In fact the first
estimate in ( 14.5 ) follows directly from Lemma
 12.1 using the estimates ( 14.1 ),
( 14.3 ) and ( 14.4 ) and the second
estimate in ( 14.5 ) follows integrating the first
from q = 1 q=1 where ( 14.2 ) holds. However, the wave
coordinate condition does not give estimates for a transversal
derivative of all components of the metric and the remaining
components have to be controlled by integrating the wave equation
expressed in polar coordinates. The estimates for the transversal
derivative obtained from the wave coordinate condition rely on a decomposition
of the metric with respect to the null
frame.
On the other hand, the estimates obtained from
integrating the wave equation are based on a decomposition of the wave
operator in terms of tangential derivatives and a transversal
derivative. 
 
 
 14.1 Proof of ( 14.3 ) and ( 14.4 ) 

 
 For a fixed angular variable ω \omega 
we integrate in the radial direction and use ( 14.1 )
and ( 14.2 ) 
 

 
 (14.9) 
 
 | Z I ​ h ​ ( t , r , ω ) | ≤ | Z I ​ h ​ ( t , t + 1 , ω ) | + ∫ r t + 1 | ∂ r Z I ​ h ​ ( t , ρ , ω ) | ​ 𝑑 ρ ≲ C ​ ε 1 + t + ∫ r t + 1 C ​ ε ​ d ​ ρ ( 1 + t + | t − ρ | ) 1 / 2 + γ ​ ( 1 + | t − ρ | ) 1 / 2 + γ ≲ C ​ ε 1 + t + C ​ ε ​ ( 1 + | t − r | ) 1 / 2 + γ ( 1 + t + r ) 1 / 2 + γ |Z^{I}h(t,r,\omega)|\leq|Z^{I}h(t,t+1,\omega)|+\int_{r}^{t+1}|\partial_{r}Z^{I}h(t,\rho,\omega)|\,d\rho\\
\lesssim\frac{C\varepsilon}{1+t}+\int_{r}^{t+1}\frac{C\varepsilon\,d\rho}{(1+t+|t-\rho|)^{1/2+\gamma}(1+|t-\rho|)^{1/2+\gamma}}\lesssim\frac{C\varepsilon}{1+t}+\frac{C\varepsilon(1+|t-r|)^{1/2+\gamma}}{(1+t+r)^{1/2+\gamma}} 
 
 
 The estimate ( 14.3 ) now follows. By Lemma
 7.2 and ( 14.3 ) 
 

 
 
 | ∂ ¯ ​ Z I ​ h | ≲ 1 1 + t + | q | ​ ∑ | J | ≤ | I | + 1 | Z J ​ h | ≲ ε ​ ( 1 + | q | ) 1 / 2 − γ ( 1 + t + | q | ) 3 / 2 + γ |\overline{\partial}Z^{I}h|\lesssim\frac{1}{1+t+|q|}\sum_{|J|\leq|I|+1}|Z^{J}h|\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon(1+|q|)^{1/2-\gamma}}{(1+t+|q|)^{3/2+\gamma}} 
 
 
 which proves ( 14.4 ).

 15 Energy estimates for Einstein’s equations 

 
 Recall the definitions 
 

 
 (15.1) 
 
 E N ​ ( t ) = sup 0 ≤ τ ≤ t ∑ | I | ≤ N ∫ Σ t | ∂ Z I ​ h | 2 , \displaystyle E_{N}(t)=\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\sum_{|I|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}|\partial Z^{I}h|^{2},

 In this section we prove the following theorem.

 Assume that g = h + m g=h+m satisfies both Einstein’s equations and the wave coordinate condition
for 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T . Suppose also that for some 0 < γ ≤ 1 / 2 0<\gamma\leq 1/2 we have the following estimates for 0 ≤ t ≤ T 0\leq t\leq T :

 The inhomogeneous term. 
By ( 13.12 )

 16 Geodesic completeness 

 
 Having constructed a solution metric g = m + h g=m+h of the Einstein
equations we need to verify that the resulting space-time ( ℝ 4 , g ) ({\mathbb{R}}^{4},g) 
is causally geodesically complete.
Let 
 

 
 
 X ⁡ ( τ ) = ( x 0 ​ ( τ ) , x ⁡ ( τ ) ) = ( t ⁡ ( τ ) , x ⁡ ( τ ) = ( t ⁡ ( τ ) , r ​ ω ​ ( τ ) ) CLOSE X(\tau)=(x^{0}(\tau),x(\tau))=(t(\tau),x(\tau)=(t(\tau),r\omega(\tau)) 
 
 
 be a causal geodesic parameterized by the affine parameter τ \tau .
Such geodesics satisfy the equations 
 

 
 (16.1) 
 
 
 X ¨ α ​ ( τ ) + Γ β ​ γ α ​ ( X ⁡ ( τ ) ) ​ X ˙ β ​ X ˙ γ = 0 , \displaystyle\ddot{X}^{\alpha}(\tau)+\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\beta\gamma}(X(\tau))\dot{X}^{\beta}\dot{X}^{\gamma}=0, 
 
 
 
 
 X ​ ( 0 ) = Y , X ˙ ​ ( 0 ) = ξ \displaystyle X(0)=Y,\quad\dot{X}(0)=\xi 
 
 
 where Y Y is the point of the origin of the geodesic X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) and
 ξ \xi is the initial velocity satisfying the condition 
 

 
 (16.2) 
 
 g α ​ β ​ ( Y ) ​ ξ α ​ ξ β = − A 2 ≤ 0 g_{\alpha\beta}(Y)\xi^{\alpha}\xi^{\beta}=-A^{2}\leq 0 
 
 
 for some constant A A . Condition ( 16.2 ) is preserved
in time, i.e., 
 

 
 (16.3)

 In the following lemma we show that a
vector η \eta causal with respect to the metric g g is ”almost”
causal with respect to the Minkowski metric m m .

 Let η \eta be a causal 4-vector, i.e, 
 

 
 (16.4)

Then 
 

 
 (16.5) 
 
 A + | η i | ≤ 2 | η 0 | , ∀ i = 1 , . . , 3 A+|\eta^{i}|\leq 2|\eta^{0}|,\qquad\forall i=1,..,3

 − | η 0 | 2 + ∑ i = 1 3 | η i | 2 ≤ | h | ⋅ ( | η 0 | 2 + ∑ i = 1 3 | η i | 2 ) -|\eta^{0}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}|\eta^{i}|^{2}\leq|h|\cdot(|\eta^{0}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}|\eta^{i}|^{2}) 
 
 
 and the desired estimate follows provided that
 | h | ≤ 1 / 4 |h|\leq 1/4 .
∎ 
 
 
 
 We choose a future oriented initial velocity ξ \xi ,
i.e., x ˙ 0 ​ ( 0 ) > 0 \dot{x}^{0}(0)>0 . 
 
 
 Proposition 16.2 . 
 
 Assume that h = g − m h=g-m satisfies the estimates 14 14

 | h | ​ | ∂ h | + | ∂ h | T ​ U + | ∂ ¯ ​ h | L ¯ ​ L ¯ ≲ ε ​ t − 1 , \displaystyle|h||\partial h|+|\partial h|_{TU}+|\bar{\partial}h|_{\underline{L}\underline{L}}\lesssim\varepsilon t^{-1}, 
 
 
 
 
 | ∂ h ⁡ ( t , x ) | ≲ ε ​ t − 1 , for | x | ≤ t / 2 \displaystyle|\partial h(t,x)|\lesssim\varepsilon t^{-1},\quad\text{for}\quad|x|\leq t/2

values of the affine parameter τ \tau span the interval [ 0 , ∞ ) [0,\infty) . 
 
 
 
 Proof.

Reparameterizing, if necessary, we can assume that the constant

 (16.6)

 We removed the absolute value from x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) \dot{x}^{0}(\tau) , since x ˙ 0 ​ ( 0 ) > 0 \dot{x}^{0}(0)>0 . This is the only part of

 Assume that X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) is a time-like geodesic of finite

 We now address the issue of null geodesics X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) , 
 

 
 
 g α ​ β ​ X ˙ α ​ X ˙ β = 0 g^{\alpha\beta}\dot{X}^{\alpha}\dot{X}^{\beta}=0

is suffices to establish that the condition

 Lemma 16.3 . 
 
 For a future oriented inextendible null geodesic X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) defined
on the interval [ 0 , τ ∗ ) [0,\tau_{*}) we have x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) > 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)>0 
for all τ ∈ [ 0 , τ ∗ ) \tau\in[0,\tau_{*}) . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Let τ 0 < τ ∗ \tau_{0}<\tau_{*} be the first time when x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) = 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})=0 .
Fix a sufficiently small small constant c c . Then there exists
a small interval of size δ \delta such that 
 

 
 
 0 ≤ x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) ≤ c , ∀ τ ∈ [ τ 0 − δ , τ 0 ] 0\leq\dot{x}^{0}(\tau)\leq c,\qquad\forall\tau\in[\tau_{0}-\delta,\tau_{0}] 
 
 
 and 
 

 
 (16.9) 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 − δ ) = c \dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0}-\delta)=c 
 
 
 We observe that ( 16.5 ) with A = 0 A=0 implies that
 | X ˙ ​ ( τ ) | ≤ 2 ​ | x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) | |\dot{X}(\tau)|\leq 2|\dot{x}^{0}(\tau)| and therefore, 
 

 
 
 | X ˙ ​ ( τ ) | ≤ 2 ​ c , ∀ τ ∈ [ τ 0 − δ , τ 0 ] |\dot{X}(\tau)|\leq 2c,\qquad\forall\tau\in[\tau_{0}-\delta,\tau_{0}] 
 
 
 Integrating the geodesic equation ( 16.1 ) we obtain 
 

 
 
 | x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) − x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 − δ ) | ≤ ∫ τ 0 − δ τ 0 | Γ | ​ | X ˙ | 2 ≤ ε ​ c 2 ​ δ |\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})-\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0}-\delta)|\leq\int_{\tau_{0}-\delta}^{\tau_{0}}|\Gamma||\dot{X}|^{2}\leq\varepsilon c^{2}\delta 
 
 
 Thus, using ( 16.9 ), 
 

 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) ≥ c − ε ​ c 2 ​ δ > 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})\geq c-\varepsilon c^{2}\delta>0 
 
 
 Contradiction.
∎ 
 
 
 
 This completes the proof of Proposition 16.2 . 
 
 
 We have shown that all future inextendible causal geodesics X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) exist
for all values of the affine parameter τ ∈ [ 0 , ∞ ) \tau\in[0,\infty) . This
means that the constructed space-time is future causally geodesically
complete. Next we establish that all future oriented causal geodesics
escape to infinity. 
 
 
 Proposition 16.4 . 
 
 Let X ⁡ ( τ ) X(\tau) be a future oriented causal geodesic. Then 
 

 
 (16.10) 
 
 lim τ → ∞ | X ⁡ ( τ ) | = ∞ \lim_{\tau\to\infty}|X(\tau)|=\infty 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 The inequality ( 16.6 ) immediately gives the desired result
for time-like geodesics.
Recall that by Lemma 16.3 we have that
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) > 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)>0 and thus x 0 ​ ( τ ) x^{0}(\tau) is monotonically increasing
in τ \tau . We now argue by contradiction.
Assume that for all τ ≥ 0 \tau\geq 0 
 

 
 
 | X ⁡ ( τ ) | ≤ C |X(\tau)|\leq C 
 
 
 for some potentially large constant C C . Then
there exists a time t 0 t_{0} such that 
 

 
 
 t 0 = lim τ → ∞ x 0 ​ ( τ ) t_{0}=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}x^{0}(\tau) 
 
 
 Set τ 0 \tau_{0} be the value of the proper time τ \tau for which
 t ⁡ ( τ 0 ) = t 0 − δ t(\tau_{0})=t_{0}-\delta for some small constant δ \delta .
Integrating the geodesic equation we obtain that for τ ≥ τ 0 \tau\geq\tau_{0} 
 

 
 (16.11) 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) = x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) + ∫ τ 0 τ | Γ | ​ | x ˙ 0 | 2 ​ d ​ τ ′ ≤ x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) + ε ​ ∫ t 0 − δ t x ˙ 0 ​ d ​ t ′ ≤ x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) + ε ​ δ ​ sup τ 0 ≤ τ ′ ≤ τ x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)=\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}|\Gamma||\dot{x}^{0}|^{2}\,d\tau^{\prime}\leq\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})+\varepsilon\int_{t_{0}-\delta}^{t}\dot{x}^{0}\,dt^{\prime}\leq\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})+\varepsilon\delta\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau}\dot{x}^{0}(\tau) 
 
 
 Thus for any τ ≥ τ 0 \tau\geq\tau_{0} 
 

 
 (16.12) 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) ≤ 2 ​ x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)\leq 2\dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0}) 
 
 
 Choosing a sequence of times τ 0 → ∞ \tau_{0}\to\infty such that
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) → 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})\to 0 (such a sequence must exist, otherwise
 x 0 ​ ( τ ) → ∞ x^{0}(\tau)\to\infty ) we infer from ( 16.12 ) that 
 

 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) → 0 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)\to 0 
 
 
 as τ → ∞ \tau\to\infty .
We can then choose small constant c , δ c,\delta such that
 t ⁡ ( τ 0 ) = t 0 − δ t(\tau_{0})=t_{0}-\delta and 
 

 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ 0 ) = c , x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) ≤ c \dot{x}^{0}(\tau_{0})=c,\qquad\dot{x}^{0}(\tau)\leq c 
 
 
 for all τ ≥ τ 0 \tau\geq\tau_{0} .
Returning to ( 16.11 ) we see that 
 

 
 
 | x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) − c | ≤ ε ​ δ ​ c |\dot{x}^{0}(\tau)-c|\leq\varepsilon\delta c 
 
 
 Thus 
 

 
 
 x ˙ 0 ​ ( τ ) ≥ c 2 \dot{x}^{0}(\tau)\geq\frac{c}{2} 
 
 
 for all τ ≥ τ 0 \tau\geq\tau_{0} and we obtained contradiction.
∎
